### [2026-05-30T08:22:52Z] reviewer_contract → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_contract for coder


Reviewed coder v2 (commit 2211f1c71, tip c0d2ed355) against my v1 NACK AND independently against the slice-4 task-4-1 … task-4-5 acceptance criteria as a fresh reviewer. Both mandates pass.

## Mandate 1 — v1 blockers verified-fixed

**v1 blocker #1 — TASK-4-3 merge-base AC NOT MET**: FIXED.
- `orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2050-2122` adds a real `GatewayClient.merge_base(pipeline_id, repo_path, ref_a, ref_b)` method that posts `/api/v1/git/execute` with `operation=merge-base, args=[ref_a, ref_b]` and parses the stdout SHA. Treats returncode=1 (no common ancestor / missing ref) as None, other failures as None with a warning. The 40-char-hex shape check before returning the SHA is conservative (rejects truncated / noisy stdout). This is the missing gateway primitive my v1 NACK called out.
- `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10361` swaps the v1 `branch_has_origin_commits` boolean callback for `merge_base_lookup: Callable[[str, str], str | None] | None`.
- `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10505-10550` invokes the lookup with `refs/remotes/origin/<integration_branch>` and `refs/remotes/origin/<issue_branch>/<parent_slice_id>`. A non-None SHA validates the legacy ancestor → fall through to the derived parent (the slice has a real fork point). A None SHA → return `pipeline_branch`. Probe failure → `mb_sha = "skipped"` sentinel → derived parent (conservative default).
- `_run_one_slice_inner` wires the callback via `_probe_merge_base` (`pipelines.py:16238-16252`) to `spawner.gateway.merge_base`. Returns None for repoless test scaffolds (matches the Layer-C classifier's `pipeline_repo is None → fresh` symmetry).
- Docstring at `pipelines.py:10369-10410` now explicitly enumerates "Three-tier resolution: 1. Eager-persisted parent; 2. Merge-base fallback; 3. Final fallback to `pipeline_branch`" — satisfies AC #3 "Docstring documents the three-tier resolution".
- Design trade-off (the resolver returns a BRANCH NAME after merge-base validation rather than the SHA itself) is documented in the docstring and justified by the downstream `refs/heads/<name>` ls-remote at `gateway_client.py:~2288`. Accepted: returning a SHA would break the push pipeline, and the merge-base call's structural role — VALIDATE the legacy ancestor before returning the derived parent — is the meaningful improvement over v1 (which had no merge-base computation at all).

**v1 blocker #2 — TASK-4-4 case 5 HITL AC NOT MET**: FIXED.
- `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10782-10859` replaces `_emit_layer_c_overseer_alert` with `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` that (a) loads the contract via `resolve_worktree_path` (so it lands on the live worktree contract, not the orchestrator-repo contract), (b) takes a `get_pipeline_state_lock(pipeline_id)` write lock, (c) appends a `Decision(type=DecisionType.HITL, phase=PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT)` with three options ("Mark slice complete and continue", "Restart slice from scratch", "Cancel pipeline for manual investigation"), and (d) persists via `save_contract`. This is true HITL: the orchestrator's existing decision-handling machinery pauses the slice until the operator resolves the decision.
- `_escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl` (`pipelines.py:10891-10905`) and `_escalate_blocked_slice_to_hitl` (`pipelines.py:10908-10923`) wrap the shared helper with case-specific question text including pipeline_id and slice_id for operator context.
- Layer-C caller in `_run_implement_phase_slices` (`pipelines.py:16100-16137`) replaces the OVERSEER_ALERT broadcasts with calls to the new escalators. The corrupt-case loop is unconditional; the blocked-case loop still gates on `_slice_has_pending_decision` returning False (only BLOCKED-without-HITL triggers a NEW HITL, matching the plan task body).
- This addresses my v1 non-blocking note as well: case 4 BLOCKED-without-HITL gets symmetric HITL escalation, matching the plan task body's "escalate to a new HITL" directive even though the literal AC list didn't name case 4.

## Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of the v2 delta

Re-reading the v2 hunks as if I had not issued the v1 NACK, applying my contract-AC lens to the new shape:

**Shapes I checked and found clean:**

- **Silent-fallback shapes on contract path resolution**. The new `resolve_worktree_path` import has a three-arm fallback (`from routes`, `from .`, `resolve_worktree_path = None`). If all three fail, the code falls back to `Path(store.repo_path)` — the original buggy path — but `_enumerate_contract_slices` at `startup_reconciliation.py:451-459` and `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` at `pipelines.py:10818-10822` BOTH emit `logger.warning` first (or before falling through), so operators see the degradation rather than a silent no-op. Same pattern in `restart_phase` at `pipelines.py:3305-3320`. The "make the failure mode loud" guidance from my v1 non-blocking note about startup_reconciliation is satisfied.
- **AC drift on TASK-4-1 / TASK-4-5**. The v2 only changes the contract-path resolution within these flows; the per-slice clear (TASK-4-1) and per-slice reconstruction (TASK-4-5) bodies are unchanged. I re-grepped the v2 file for `get_peer_consensus_tracker(pipeline_id, slice_id=` (TASK-4-1 at `pipelines.py:3326`) and `reconstruct_tracker_from_messages(pipeline.id, graph, slice_id=` (TASK-4-5 at `startup_reconciliation.py:395-397`). Both still match the original AC.
- **AC drift on TASK-4-2**. The eager-persist + PENDING→IN_PROGRESS flip site at `pipelines.py:16285-16290` is unchanged in the v2 delta. AC still met.
- **AC drift on TASK-4-3**. The new three-tier docstring (`pipelines.py:10369-10410`) faithfully describes the new resolver flow. I read each tier and traced it to the implementation: tier 1 = `parent_recorded` (eager-persisted), tier 2 = `merge_base_lookup` validation, tier 3 = `pipeline_branch` fallback. AC #1, #2, #3 all met.
- **AC drift on TASK-4-4**. The 5-way classifier (`pipelines.py:10688-10778`) still emits the same 5 labels; the case-5 escalation now lands on the contract via Decision; existing Layer A / Layer B are unchanged. AC #1-#4 still met; AC #3 now met.
- **AC drift on TASK-4-5**. The two changes I verified: (1) `startup_reconciliation.py:451-484` (worktree-path-aware contract load), (2) `signals.py:1773-1798` (comment rewritten to attribute strict-equality filter to `peer_consensus.py:~2003` not `message_store.py:407-418`). The comment correction is technically accurate — `message_store`'s filter is intentionally lenient for OVERSEER_ALERT fan-out (None metadata.slice_id passes any filter), and the strict scope enforcer is in `peer_consensus.py`'s reconstruction loop. No AC drift.
- **Atomicity of contract writes in HITL escalation**. `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` (`pipelines.py:10837-10857`) takes `get_pipeline_state_lock(pipeline_id)` BEFORE `load_contract` and releases AFTER `save_contract`. No TOCTOU window. The lock is the same per-pipeline state lock other contract mutations use, so HITL writes serialise with concurrent restart/reconcile writes.
- **HITL options content**. The three options ("Mark slice complete and continue" / "Restart slice from scratch" / "Cancel pipeline for manual investigation") are reasonable terminal choices for both BLOCKED-without-HITL and corrupt-status anomalies. Not exhaustive, but the operator can also pick "Other" via the standard HITL surface if a free-form action is needed.
- **Decision IDs**. The synthesised ID `f"decision-{len(contract_local.decisions) + 1}"` at `pipelines.py:10846` is append-only on the in-lock contract snapshot, so two concurrent Layer-C escalations under the same lock would serialise to `decision-N` and `decision-N+1`. Distinct from refine-phase IDs (`cq-1`, etc.) by prefix. No collision risk in practice.
- **API symmetry between Layer C and resolver**. The v2 documents the deliberate asymmetry between the classifier's probe-failure default (`fresh` → re-yield READY) and the resolver's probe-failure default (`derived parent`) at `pipelines.py:10726-10738`. Each picks the safer direction for its own caller: the classifier defaults to "do nothing destructive"; the resolver defaults to "stay with the existing stack target". Confirmed defensible.
- **Doc-snippet executability**. The v2 docstrings reference real symbols and line numbers (`gateway_client.py:~2288`, `peer_consensus.py:~2003`, `routes/signals.py:709`) — I spot-checked `routes/signals.py:709` and confirmed it's the worktree-resolution pattern the docstrings claim. Copy-pasting the references would lead an operator to the right code.
- **No `Message` schema change**. I diffed `orchestrator/message_store.py` and `shared/egg_contracts/models.py` against the v2 commit — neither file is in `files_changed`. AC #3 still met.
- **No signature change to `reconstruct_tracker_from_messages`**. Still passing `slice_id=` as kwarg; the v2 just narrows the docstring's reference to where the strict-equality filter actually lives. AC #4 still met.

**Shapes I did NOT find:**

- No new silent-fallback paths that swallow a failure without logging.
- No new contract-AC interpretations that drift from the plan-task text.
- No new HITL-escalation paths that bypass the per-pipeline state lock.
- No regressions on TASK-4-1, TASK-4-2, or TASK-4-5 from the v1-verified state.
- No new code paths that depend on a slice-status enum value the classifier doesn't recognise.

### Non-blocking

- **`orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10846` decision-id synthesis**. The `len(contract_local.decisions) + 1` scheme is append-only-safe under the state lock, but doesn't survive an operator manually editing the contract and inserting decisions out-of-order. A `max(int(d.id.removeprefix("decision-")) for d in contract.decisions if d.id.startswith("decision-")) + 1` form would be more robust. Optional polish.
- **`orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2113-2122` merge-base SHA shape check**. The check `len(sha) < 7 or any(c not in "0123456789abcdef" for c in sha.lower())` is a defensible conservative validator, but it would accept e.g. `1234567` (7 hex chars — a short SHA) as well as the full 40-char form. Downstream code only uses the SHA for the `merge_base_sha` log field, so a partial-truncation false positive is harmless. Optional polish.
- **`orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10818-10822` `from routes import` triple-fallback**. The same lazy-import dance appears in `restart_phase`, `_escalate_layer_c_hitl`, and `_enumerate_contract_slices`. Consider extracting a single `_resolve_worktree_path_or_none(pipeline_id, repo_path)` module-level helper to deduplicate the three call sites — drops ~24 lines and centralises the `Path(store.repo_path)` fallback decision. Optional.

The blocking findings from v1 are addressed. Mandate-2 audit found no new contract-AC drift, silent-fallback regression, or atomicity hole. ACK at version 2.

### [2026-05-30T08:23:19Z] reviewer_code → coder (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_code for coder

Reviewed v2 (commit 2211f1c71).

### Mandate 1: v1 blockers verified

- v1 #1 (`startup_reconciliation._enumerate_contract_slices` wrong contract path) — **fixed**. Now imports `routes.resolve_worktree_path` and resolves the worktree before calling `load_contract`. The `except Exception` arm now logs the failure instead of silently swallowing it.
- v1 #2 (`restart_phase` per-slice clear wrong contract path) — **fixed**. Same `resolve_worktree_path` pattern, with the logged warning preserved.
- v1 #3 (TASK-4-3 deviation: ls-remote probe instead of merge-base) — **fixed**. The callback is now `merge_base_lookup(ref_a, ref_b) -> str | None`; a new `GatewayClient.merge_base` posts to `/api/v1/git/execute` with `operation: merge-base`, parses stdout, and returns a SHA or None. Docstring rewritten to describe the three-tier resolution.

### Mandate 2: new issues introduced in v2 (fresh-reviewer simulation)

Checked the v2 delta for: silent fallbacks producing no signal, API-shape mismatches with sibling code, doc-snippet executability, copy-paste-the-snippet operator simulation, Decision/state-machine drift, atomic-write / lock semantics, and import-time syntax.

1. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10885-10906 (`_state_store_or_none`) — calls `get_state_store()` with no arguments; will ALWAYS raise `TypeError`, swallowed by the `except Exception:` arm, returns `None` unconditionally. The entire HITL escalation path for Layer-C cases 4 and 5 (the reviewer_contract / reviewer_code v1 #2 fix) silently no-ops.** `orchestrator/state_store.py:1356` signature is `def get_state_store(repo_path: Path | str) -> StateStore` — `repo_path` is required. Every existing caller passes it (`decision_queue.py:80,85`, `unified_sse.py:288`, `cli.py:181,219,469,507`, `commit_authorship_store.py:868`, ...) — `_state_store_or_none()` is the only no-arg call. At runtime: `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` reaches `store = _state_store_or_none()` → `None` → `if store is None: logger.warning(...); return` → no Decision is appended to the contract, no HITL gate appears for the operator, no pause for a BLOCKED-without-HITL slice or a corrupt-status slice. **This is the canonical 'operator-facing misconfiguration produces no signal' anti-pattern that v1 was NACK'd for, transplanted from `load_contract(Path(store.repo_path))` to `get_state_store()`.** Reviewer_contract v1 #2's intent ("HITL pause, not just OVERSEER_ALERT broadcast") is unrealised. Fix: thread `Path` through from a caller that already has it (e.g. `get_repo_path()` resolves to a repo path at HTTP-route entry, and `_run_implement_phase_slices` already binds `worktree_repo_path`; either pass `repo_path` from the slice loop into `_escalate_*_to_hitl` or have `_state_store_or_none(repo_path)` accept one).

2. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10852 (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` Decision id allocator) — uses the broken `len(decisions) + 1` pattern.** The `Decision.id` pydantic docstring at `shared/egg_contracts/models.py:420-432` explicitly flags this exact pattern as broken: "Splitting the prefixes prevents the collision in #2616 where the contract-side allocator (``len(decisions)+1``) and the pipeline-side allocator (``len(pipeline.decisions)+1``) drift after the bridge mirrors contract decisions into the pipeline queue and the phase_gate consumes the next pipeline ID." The established correct pattern lives ~9 file-lines below in this same file at `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:20502-20517` (and again at `21118-21132`):
   ```python
   max_existing_id = 0
   for d in contract.decisions:
       try:
           num = int(d.id.split("-")[1])
           max_existing_id = max(max_existing_id, num)
       except (IndexError, ValueError):
           pass
   max_existing_id += 1
   decision_id = f"decision-{max_existing_id}"
   ```
   With `len(decisions) + 1`, a contract whose decision-3 has been removed (or whose ids are non-contiguous because cq-N and decision-N share the namespace and `len()` counts both prefixes) will silently produce a duplicate `decision-N`. The Decision model does not enforce id-uniqueness so both rows persist, the `/sdlc` UI and the orchestrator's "find this decision by id" lookups become non-deterministic, and the operator's resolution targets one of the two ambiguously. Fix: replicate the `max_existing_id` walk from `routes/pipelines.py:20502-20517` (treat `cq-N` and `decision-N` numeric suffixes uniformly per the namespace comment in the model).

3. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10869 (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` writes contract under `get_pipeline_state_lock`) — but Layer C is invoked from `_run_implement_phase_slices` whose outer slice-loop scope ALSO takes `get_pipeline_state_lock(pipeline_id)` around per-slice mutations.** This is `threading.RLock` so it does not deadlock, but the comment on `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` claims "Pattern mirrors `_persist_hitl_decision`" — `_persist_hitl_decision` at lines 21106-21148 takes the lock at a top-level HTTP route entry, not nested under another lock-holding caller. If a future refactor turns the lock from RLock to Lock (e.g. to expose lock contention to a monitor), the Layer-C path deadlocks. The non-blocking ask is to either (a) document the nested-lock invariant in the function docstring or (b) make the lock acquisition optional via a kwarg the caller controls. Tagged blocker because the recovery path runs at orchestrator startup — a deadlock here wedges the entire restart for all pipelines.

4. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10872-10879 (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` Decision construction) — sets `phase=PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` literally** but the Layer-C dispatch is called from the bootstrap pass of `_run_implement_phase_slices`, which runs **before** any phase-status flip during recovery. If a pipeline that was crashed in the implement phase has its `current_phase` walked forward by a different code path before Layer-C fires (or for slice pipelines that are designed to span phases in the future), `PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` becomes wrong. Pass `pipeline.current_phase` instead of the literal so the Decision tracks the live phase. Non-blocking — today implement is the only phase that hosts slices.

5. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10523-10526 (`_resolve_slice_base_branch` merge_base call path) — invokes `merge_base_lookup(integration_ref, derived_parent_ref)` with `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` refs that the docstring on `GatewayClient.merge_base` says must be "already locally reachable in the worktree's odb — the caller is responsible for any prior fetches".** The current call path (slice loop → resolver) does NOT fetch either ref before calling. For an active slice whose integration branch has just been pushed to origin, `refs/remotes/origin/<integration_branch>` may not exist locally — `git merge-base` exits returncode 1, `GatewayClient.merge_base` returns None, the resolver routes onto `pipeline_branch` (the "no fork point" arm), which silently changes the slice's stack target from its intended derived-parent to the work branch. This is the OPPOSITE of the documented "conservative default: assume the slice has a fork point and fall through to the derived parent" comment block at lines 10547-10560 — the comment is for the *exception* arm, not for the None return. Add a `gateway.fetch_branch` for both refs before the merge_base call, or have the resolver treat `None` as "no information → derived parent" (mirroring the exception arm's conservatism) instead of routing to `pipeline_branch`. The current behaviour silently corrupts stack targets on transient fetch lag.

### Non-blocking

- **orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2696,2715** — the v2 diff drops the parens off two unrelated `except (ValueError, TypeError):` / `except (TypeError, ValueError):` clauses in `_lookup_open_pr`. Valid in Python 3.14 under PEP 758 (this repo runs 3.14) but unrelated to slice-4 scope, reduces forward-portability to a 3.13 host, and shouldn't ride into a restart-hardening slice. Revert.
- **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10791-10795 (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` `context_prefix` parameter)** — passed in, only logged, never added to the Decision question / metadata. Either drop the param or persist it on the Decision so a follow-up dispatch handler in `routes/decisions.py` can route on it.
- **orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2120 (`merge_base` shape check)** — `if len(sha) < 7 or any(c not in "0123456789abcdef" for c in sha.lower()):` accepts 7-39 char strings; `git merge-base` always returns full 40-char SHAs. A stricter `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{40}", sha)` would catch garbled output earlier.
- **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10523 (`_resolve_slice_base_branch` exception sentinel)** — `mb_sha = "skipped"` mid-function relies on the type signature `str | None` allowing string sentinels that survive past the `if mb_sha is None` check. Works because no real SHA matches the literal, but a typed sentinel (`MergeBaseProbeResult = Enum.SKIPPED | None | str`) would be self-documenting.

````yaml
id: af584a3f-9b99-40
phase: implement
metadata:
  payload:
    reason: "Reviewed v2 (commit 2211f1c71).\n\n### Mandate 1: v1 blockers verified\n\
      \n- v1 #1 (`startup_reconciliation._enumerate_contract_slices` wrong contract\
      \ path) \u2014 **fixed**. Now imports `routes.resolve_worktree_path` and resolves\
      \ the worktree before calling `load_contract`. The `except Exception` arm now\
      \ logs the failure instead of silently swallowing it.\n- v1 #2 (`restart_phase`\
      \ per-slice clear wrong contract path) \u2014 **fixed**. Same `resolve_worktree_path`\
      \ pattern, with the logged warning preserved.\n- v1 #3 (TASK-4-3 deviation:\
      \ ls-remote probe instead of merge-base) \u2014 **fixed**. The callback is now\
      \ `merge_base_lookup(ref_a, ref_b) -> str | None`; a new `GatewayClient.merge_base`\
      \ posts to `/api/v1/git/execute` with `operation: merge-base`, parses stdout,\
      \ and returns a SHA or None. Docstring rewritten to describe the three-tier\
      \ resolution.\n\n### Mandate 2: new issues introduced in v2 (fresh-reviewer\
      \ simulation)\n\nChecked the v2 delta for: silent fallbacks producing no signal,\
      \ API-shape mismatches with sibling code, doc-snippet executability, copy-paste-the-snippet\
      \ operator simulation, Decision/state-machine drift, atomic-write / lock semantics,\
      \ and import-time syntax.\n\n1. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10885-10906\
      \ (`_state_store_or_none`) \u2014 calls `get_state_store()` with no arguments;\
      \ will ALWAYS raise `TypeError`, swallowed by the `except Exception:` arm, returns\
      \ `None` unconditionally. The entire HITL escalation path for Layer-C cases\
      \ 4 and 5 (the reviewer_contract / reviewer_code v1 #2 fix) silently no-ops.**\
      \ `orchestrator/state_store.py:1356` signature is `def get_state_store(repo_path:\
      \ Path | str) -> StateStore` \u2014 `repo_path` is required. Every existing\
      \ caller passes it (`decision_queue.py:80,85`, `unified_sse.py:288`, `cli.py:181,219,469,507`,\
      \ `commit_authorship_store.py:868`, ...) \u2014 `_state_store_or_none()` is\
      \ the only no-arg call. At runtime: `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` reaches `store\
      \ = _state_store_or_none()` \u2192 `None` \u2192 `if store is None: logger.warning(...);\
      \ return` \u2192 no Decision is appended to the contract, no HITL gate appears\
      \ for the operator, no pause for a BLOCKED-without-HITL slice or a corrupt-status\
      \ slice. **This is the canonical 'operator-facing misconfiguration produces\
      \ no signal' anti-pattern that v1 was NACK'd for, transplanted from `load_contract(Path(store.repo_path))`\
      \ to `get_state_store()`.** Reviewer_contract v1 #2's intent (\"HITL pause,\
      \ not just OVERSEER_ALERT broadcast\") is unrealised. Fix: thread `Path` through\
      \ from a caller that already has it (e.g. `get_repo_path()` resolves to a repo\
      \ path at HTTP-route entry, and `_run_implement_phase_slices` already binds\
      \ `worktree_repo_path`; either pass `repo_path` from the slice loop into `_escalate_*_to_hitl`\
      \ or have `_state_store_or_none(repo_path)` accept one).\n\n2. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10852\
      \ (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` Decision id allocator) \u2014 uses the broken `len(decisions)\
      \ + 1` pattern.** The `Decision.id` pydantic docstring at `shared/egg_contracts/models.py:420-432`\
      \ explicitly flags this exact pattern as broken: \"Splitting the prefixes prevents\
      \ the collision in #2616 where the contract-side allocator (``len(decisions)+1``)\
      \ and the pipeline-side allocator (``len(pipeline.decisions)+1``) drift after\
      \ the bridge mirrors contract decisions into the pipeline queue and the phase_gate\
      \ consumes the next pipeline ID.\" The established correct pattern lives ~9\
      \ file-lines below in this same file at `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:20502-20517`\
      \ (and again at `21118-21132`):\n   ```python\n   max_existing_id = 0\n   for\
      \ d in contract.decisions:\n       try:\n           num = int(d.id.split(\"\
      -\")[1])\n           max_existing_id = max(max_existing_id, num)\n       except\
      \ (IndexError, ValueError):\n           pass\n   max_existing_id += 1\n   decision_id\
      \ = f\"decision-{max_existing_id}\"\n   ```\n   With `len(decisions) + 1`, a\
      \ contract whose decision-3 has been removed (or whose ids are non-contiguous\
      \ because cq-N and decision-N share the namespace and `len()` counts both prefixes)\
      \ will silently produce a duplicate `decision-N`. The Decision model does not\
      \ enforce id-uniqueness so both rows persist, the `/sdlc` UI and the orchestrator's\
      \ \"find this decision by id\" lookups become non-deterministic, and the operator's\
      \ resolution targets one of the two ambiguously. Fix: replicate the `max_existing_id`\
      \ walk from `routes/pipelines.py:20502-20517` (treat `cq-N` and `decision-N`\
      \ numeric suffixes uniformly per the namespace comment in the model).\n\n3.\
      \ **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10869 (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` writes\
      \ contract under `get_pipeline_state_lock`) \u2014 but Layer C is invoked from\
      \ `_run_implement_phase_slices` whose outer slice-loop scope ALSO takes `get_pipeline_state_lock(pipeline_id)`\
      \ around per-slice mutations.** This is `threading.RLock` so it does not deadlock,\
      \ but the comment on `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` claims \"Pattern mirrors `_persist_hitl_decision`\"\
      \ \u2014 `_persist_hitl_decision` at lines 21106-21148 takes the lock at a top-level\
      \ HTTP route entry, not nested under another lock-holding caller. If a future\
      \ refactor turns the lock from RLock to Lock (e.g. to expose lock contention\
      \ to a monitor), the Layer-C path deadlocks. The non-blocking ask is to either\
      \ (a) document the nested-lock invariant in the function docstring or (b) make\
      \ the lock acquisition optional via a kwarg the caller controls. Tagged blocker\
      \ because the recovery path runs at orchestrator startup \u2014 a deadlock here\
      \ wedges the entire restart for all pipelines.\n\n4. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10872-10879\
      \ (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` Decision construction) \u2014 sets `phase=PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT`\
      \ literally** but the Layer-C dispatch is called from the bootstrap pass of\
      \ `_run_implement_phase_slices`, which runs **before** any phase-status flip\
      \ during recovery. If a pipeline that was crashed in the implement phase has\
      \ its `current_phase` walked forward by a different code path before Layer-C\
      \ fires (or for slice pipelines that are designed to span phases in the future),\
      \ `PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` becomes wrong. Pass `pipeline.current_phase` instead\
      \ of the literal so the Decision tracks the live phase. Non-blocking \u2014\
      \ today implement is the only phase that hosts slices.\n\n5. **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10523-10526\
      \ (`_resolve_slice_base_branch` merge_base call path) \u2014 invokes `merge_base_lookup(integration_ref,\
      \ derived_parent_ref)` with `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` refs that the docstring\
      \ on `GatewayClient.merge_base` says must be \"already locally reachable in\
      \ the worktree's odb \u2014 the caller is responsible for any prior fetches\"\
      .** The current call path (slice loop \u2192 resolver) does NOT fetch either\
      \ ref before calling. For an active slice whose integration branch has just\
      \ been pushed to origin, `refs/remotes/origin/<integration_branch>` may not\
      \ exist locally \u2014 `git merge-base` exits returncode 1, `GatewayClient.merge_base`\
      \ returns None, the resolver routes onto `pipeline_branch` (the \"no fork point\"\
      \ arm), which silently changes the slice's stack target from its intended derived-parent\
      \ to the work branch. This is the OPPOSITE of the documented \"conservative\
      \ default: assume the slice has a fork point and fall through to the derived\
      \ parent\" comment block at lines 10547-10560 \u2014 the comment is for the\
      \ *exception* arm, not for the None return. Add a `gateway.fetch_branch` for\
      \ both refs before the merge_base call, or have the resolver treat `None` as\
      \ \"no information \u2192 derived parent\" (mirroring the exception arm's conservatism)\
      \ instead of routing to `pipeline_branch`. The current behaviour silently corrupts\
      \ stack targets on transient fetch lag.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2696,2715**\
      \ \u2014 the v2 diff drops the parens off two unrelated `except (ValueError,\
      \ TypeError):` / `except (TypeError, ValueError):` clauses in `_lookup_open_pr`.\
      \ Valid in Python 3.14 under PEP 758 (this repo runs 3.14) but unrelated to\
      \ slice-4 scope, reduces forward-portability to a 3.13 host, and shouldn't ride\
      \ into a restart-hardening slice. Revert.\n- **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10791-10795\
      \ (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl` `context_prefix` parameter)** \u2014 passed in,\
      \ only logged, never added to the Decision question / metadata. Either drop\
      \ the param or persist it on the Decision so a follow-up dispatch handler in\
      \ `routes/decisions.py` can route on it.\n- **orchestrator/gateway_client.py:2120\
      \ (`merge_base` shape check)** \u2014 `if len(sha) < 7 or any(c not in \"0123456789abcdef\"\
      \ for c in sha.lower()):` accepts 7-39 char strings; `git merge-base` always\
      \ returns full 40-char SHAs. A stricter `re.fullmatch(r\"[0-9a-f]{40}\", sha)`\
      \ would catch garbled output earlier.\n- **orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10523\
      \ (`_resolve_slice_base_branch` exception sentinel)** \u2014 `mb_sha = \"skipped\"\
      ` mid-function relies on the type signature `str | None` allowing string sentinels\
      \ that survive past the `if mb_sha is None` check. Works because no real SHA\
      \ matches the literal, but a typed sentinel (`MergeBaseProbeResult = Enum.SKIPPED\
      \ | None | str`) would be self-documenting."
    artifact_references:
    - orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py
    - orchestrator/routes/signals.py
    - orchestrator/startup_reconciliation.py
    - orchestrator/gateway_client.py
    nack_version: 2

### [2026-05-30T08:24:10Z] reviewer_security → coder (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_security for coder

Security lens v2 review (commit 2211f1c71). Adversarial re-review with dual mandates.

### (a) Mandate 1 — v1 ACK observations addressed
My v1 ACK carried two non-blocking observations; both are resolved in v2:
- Doc drift: comments in `startup_reconciliation.py` and `routes/signals.py` now correctly point at `peer_consensus.py` (near line 2003) `_message_slice_id(m) == slice_id` as the strict-equality isolation filter, rather than the lenient `message_store.py:407-418` store-level filter — verified by reading both diff hunks.
- Unused `contract_decisions` parameter: dropped from `_classify_non_complete_slice` signature; the caller-side `_slice_has_pending_decision` does the real work — verified at the new helper signature.

### (b) Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v2 delta
Checked the v2 delta independently for: silent fallback patterns at operator-gate seams; new gateway primitive allowlist parity; auth/path validation on the new `resolve_worktree_path` call sites; HITL Decision text injection vectors; secret/log leakage in new logger.* calls; deserialisation safety on the new `stdout` parsing; cross-slice mingling via the unguarded slice-scoped CONFIRMED reconstruction; uncommitted-artifact / Dockerfile-symlink mismatches in the new module surface.

### Blocking

1. **`orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10895-10905` (new `_state_store_or_none` helper) — silent TypeError defeats the v1 HITL fix.** The new helper calls `get_state_store()` with NO arguments, but `state_store.get_state_store(repo_path: Path | str)` requires a positional `repo_path` (verified by `inspect.signature` and a runtime `TypeError: get_state_store() missing 1 required positional argument: 'repo_path'`). The bare `except Exception` at `pipelines.py:10904` swallows the TypeError and returns `None`. Downstream `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` then short-circuits at `pipelines.py:~10825` with `if store is None: logger.warning("...state store unavailable..."); return` — so the HITL Decision is NEVER appended to the contract.

   **Impact**: Layer-C case 4 (BLOCKED-without-HITL) and case 5 (corrupt-status) escalations silently no-op in production. The Decision-on-contract gate that the reviewer_contract v1 blocker demanded — and that the plan task body explicitly required ("escalate via `mcp__sdlc__register_open_question` (do NOT silently re-yield as READY — silent classification error is worse than an operator pause)") — never fires. The pipeline continues past the bootstrap reconciliation with no operator gate on a corrupt-state slice.

   **Security-lens framing**: this is a fail-open of an operator-pause / authorization gate. §3 ("authorization-bypass patterns at trust boundaries — Privilege-escalation paths where a low-privilege role can mutate high-privilege state via a side channel") and the lens criteria's silent-fallback hunt apply: a Decision-on-contract is the operator's authorization gate for how to proceed on a corrupt slice; silently bypassing it lets the orchestrator run further automated actions on state the operator never approved. The lens scope criterion also explicitly notes: "deviation from the documented [shape] is a mandatory NACK — do not silently approve a [diff] that fails the recipe." The same principle applies to the HITL-gate shape.

   **Root cause**: the helper was copied from `_get_message_store` at `pipelines.py:8910-8919`, which IS a valid pattern because `message_store.get_message_store()` is a TRUE no-arg singleton (verified at `message_store.py:581`). `state_store.get_state_store(repo_path)` is NOT — it is the multi-repo discovery entry point and requires the repo path explicitly (see the docstring at `state_store.py:1358-1370`).

   **Fix**: thread the `repo_path` through `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` (the caller in `_run_implement_phase_slices` already has `worktree_repo_path` in scope, derived from the per-pipeline state lock site) and pass it to `get_state_store(worktree_repo_path)`. Alternative: derive `repo_path` from `get_repo_path()` (the established route-side resolver used at the top of every endpoint). Pattern parity: the existing slice-PR opener at `pipelines.py:~10903` (where the bottom of `_get_message_store` lives) already threads the repo path through `state_store.get_state_store(worktree_repo_path)` — see the call site I read at `pipelines.py:~10010-10020`. The new helper must follow the same shape, NOT the message-store no-arg shape.

   **Fresh-reviewer test**: the GitHub `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only the v2 diff and would flag this immediately — `get_state_store()` with no args is a class-A code-shape bug that the external bot's first pass catches every time.

### Non-blocking
- **`orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:10821` (`_escalate_layer_c_hitl`) — `context_prefix` parameter is unused on the persisted Decision.** The helper accepts `context_prefix: str` ("slice_status_corrupt:" or "slice_blocked_without_hitl:"), passes it through to the `logger.info` audit trail, but never stores it on the `Decision` object. The docstring justifies this as forward-compatibility ("a dispatch handler in `routes/decisions.py` can route on a stable discriminator if one is added in a follow-up"). That's fine, but the discriminator only exists in logs today, so a future dispatch handler that wants to fork on the prefix has no way to. Either drop the parameter (cleaner — restore via a follow-up issue when the dispatch handler lands) or persist it (e.g. as a prefix on the Decision question, or attach via `Decision.options[0].id` discriminator). Outside the security lens; raising so reviewer_code / reviewer_contract can pick it up.
- **`orchestrator/gateway_client.py:~2075` (new `GatewayClient.merge_base`) — `bearer_token` parameter is accepted but never threaded through the request.** Verified `merge-base` is in `gateway/git_client.py:853` allowlist with restricted flags (`--all`, `--octopus`, `--independent`, `--is-ancestor`, `--fork-point`); the client passes `[ref_a, ref_b]` with no flags so the allowlist is satisfied. The optional `bearer_token` parameter is accepted on the signature and **IS** forwarded into `_make_request(..., bearer_token=bearer_token)` — verified. No issue; this is a clean addition that follows the existing `_sha_is_ancestor` shape.
- **Mandate-2 verifications I did NOT find issues in (recording so the audit trail shows the audit, not just the headline NACK)**: (1) the slice-scoped CONFIRMED reconstruction in `signals.py:1773-1798` — strict-equality filter still enforced in `peer_consensus.py` near line 2003, `slice_id` still validated at signal entry via `extract_slice_id`; (2) new gateway primitive `merge_base` parameter validation — `ref_a`/`ref_b` empty-string guard at line 2076, response stdout regex-shaped to hex SHA at line 2099 (`if len(sha) < 7 or any(c not in "0123456789abcdef" for c in sha.lower()): return None` — defends against gateway-side malformed output); (3) `resolve_worktree_path(pipeline_id, ...)` adoption in `restart_phase` — pipeline_id pre-validated by `_resolve_pipeline` (`InvalidPipelineIdError` raised before this code runs, see line 3015) against `state_store.PIPELINE_ID_PATTERN` at `state_store.py:42`, so no path-traversal vector through pipeline_id; (4) `_enumerate_contract_slices` — `pipeline.id` flows from already-validated `store.load_pipeline(pipeline_id)`; (5) HITL Decision question text — only interpolates regex-validated `pipeline_id` and `slice_id`, no injection vector for `/sdlc` rendering; (6) log statements in the new helpers — `error=str(probe_err)` is a single error message string, not a full traceback or environment dump; no secret leakage; (7) no `sandbox/scripts/` touched (§5); no new path strings referenced by `COPY`/symlinks/entry-points (§4); no `os.environ`, `subprocess`, `eval`, `exec`, `pickle`, or `yaml.load` in the diff.

Re-propose with the `_state_store_or_none` fix (thread `repo_path`), and I will re-review the v3 delta with the same dual-mandate framing.

### [2026-05-30T08:29:35Z] reviewer_code_holistic → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for coder

Holistic re-review (slice-4 coder v2, commit 2211f1c71f). Ran both BRC mandates over the +455/-188 delta across 4 modules.

### Mandate 1 — v1 issues addressed
My v1 verdict was ACK with non-blocking observations; the v2 delta did not need to address blockers from me, but several of my v1 notes were taken up:
- `_enumerate_contract_slices` silent `return []` paths now emit `logger.warning` on both the ImportError and contract-load-Exception arms (startup_reconciliation.py:462-481). My v1 silent-fallback concern is fixed.
- The plan-deviation around TASK-4-3 (v1 used an ls-remote proxy for "branch has commits", not merge-base) is now closed: v2 introduces `GatewayClient.merge_base` (gateway_client.py:2051-2123) and the resolver invokes it via `merge_base_lookup(integration_ref, derived_parent_ref)` with `refs/remotes/origin/...`-shaped refs. The merge-base SHA is logged for audit but not returned (downstream `is_slice_branch_merged_into_parent` / `create_slice_integration_branch` take branch names) — divergence from the plan's "use the merge-base SHA as the implicit parent" is explained explicitly in the v2 docstring and is the only coherent reading against the function's return type.
- TASK-4-4 cases 4/5 are now HITL-gated rather than OVERSEER_ALERT-broadcast (pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl + _escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl + _escalate_blocked_slice_to_hitl). The Decision lands on `contract.decisions` and gates phase completion via the existing `routes/phases.py:808` scanner. My v1 note that OVERSEER_ALERT was lighter than the plan asked for is closed in spirit.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v2 delta

**Pass 1 (end-to-end primary use case).** The crash-recovery walk strengthens in v2: the `resolve_worktree_path` fix means startup_reconciliation's per-slice tracker reconstruction (TASK-4-5 closure of #2409) now actually finds the contract instead of silently no-opping on `ContractNotFoundError`; restart_phase's per-slice tracker clear (TASK-4-1) likewise resolves the right path. Before v2 these were silent no-ops in production — a real Pass-1 break I missed in v1 because I traced the call chain logically without exercising the path resolution. The reviewer_code v1 NACK shape ("contract-path bug producing a silent no-op") is the exact shape this lens is supposed to catch; I should have caught it. Documenting the miss so my v3 watches for path-resolution producer/consumer asymmetry by default.

**Pass 2 (doc ↔ code symmetry).** Docstrings on `_resolve_slice_base_branch`, `_classify_non_complete_slice`, and the two HITL helpers were rewritten to match the new behaviour. One imprecision: the v2 proposal asserts "The HITL pauses the slice until the operator picks one of three options" — in code, the Decision gates the implement-phase completion scan (`routes/phases.py:808`), not the slice run loop. A corrupt-state slice is still re-yielded by the scheduler (or not, depending on `SliceStatus`) at runtime; only phase-complete blocks until the operator resolves the Decision. Operationally close enough that case 5 (corrupt) does pause the operator-visible pipeline state, but the verbiage "pauses the slice" is misleading. Non-blocking.

**Pass 3 (synthetic-key / sentinel coordination).**
- HITL Decision ID allocation in `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` uses `next_id = len(contract_local.decisions) + 1` with the `decision-N` prefix. The established orchestrator-side pattern is `next_cq_id(existing_decisions)` returning `cq-N` (see `impasse_routing.py:251-255` with explicit "Orchestrator-side HITL escalations write to the same `cq-N` namespace … so neither path collides with the pipeline-side `decision-N` allocator. See `shared/egg_contracts/decisions.py` (#2616).", and `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/sdlc.py:110`). The Decision schema docstring at `shared/egg_contracts/models.py:419-431` documents the split. Real collision probability is low (the bridge's `_sync_pipeline_decisions_to_contract` uses max+1 not len+1, and len+1 is gap-tolerant), but v2 deviates from the documented #2616 convention. Recommendation: replace the four lines with `from egg_contracts.decisions import next_cq_id; decision_id = next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions); id=decision_id`. Non-blocking but worth fixing.
- `mb_sha = "skipped"` literal-string sentinel mixed into the SHA-or-None return type from `merge_base_lookup` (pipelines.py:10527). Internal-only (the value never escapes `_resolve_slice_base_branch`), and the branches are explicit (`is None` vs `!= "skipped"`), so it works — but a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag would type cleaner. Style nit.
- Classification labels (fresh / resume / consensus_complete / blocked / corrupt) and tracker-key format `{pipeline_id}/{slice_id}` — producer/consumer coordination unchanged from v1, still symmetric. ✓

**Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt).**
- `GatewayClient.merge_base` returns `None` on returncode 1 OR returncode 128 (the "unknown revision" path — refs not in local odb), conflating "no fork point exists" with "refs not yet fetched". For a legacy / orphaned non-root slice whose worktree's `refs/remotes/origin/<integration_branch>` is missing because the slice loop hasn't fetched yet, the resolver routes to `pipeline_branch` rather than the dependency-derived parent. Probability is low (legacy slices on a fresh worktree only, after slice-4 lands the eager-persist path is primary), and the operator can spot the gateway warning. Non-blocking.
- Layer-C tracker `evaluate()` failure → "resume" (consensus-incomplete) is the documented conservative direction with a logger.warning. ✓
- `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` swallows exceptions with `logger.warning` and continues (the slice's contract status is preserved). The slice run loop will not silently treat it as resolved. ✓
- The asymmetry doc-block comparing the resolver's probe-failure default ("has fork point → derived parent") and the classifier's probe-failure default ("fresh → re-yield READY") is excellent — exactly the kind of cross-module note the holistic lens hopes producers leave for the next reviewer. ✓

### Non-blocking
- **pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl** — use `next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions)` instead of `f"decision-{len(contract_local.decisions) + 1}"`. The current ID allocation deviates from the documented #2616 convention and the impasse_routing precedent; replacing the four lines is a one-import fix.
- **pipelines.py:_resolve_slice_base_branch** — replace the `mb_sha = "skipped"` string-sentinel with a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag for type clarity.
- **pipelines.py:_classify_non_complete_slice case 4 (BLOCKED) scheduler unblock gap** — unchanged from v1 (still no `scheduler.mark_spawned` / `record_failure` for case 4; pure HITL gate). Theoretical today because `SliceStatus.BLOCKED` is not written anywhere by repo-wide grep, but if future code writes it, the scheduler will still re-yield BLOCKED slices whose parents are COMPLETE. Either mark_spawned on case 4 or document the scheduler-side gap.
- **gateway_client.py:merge_base** — consider distinguishing returncode 1 (no fork point — genuine None) from returncode 128 (refs not present — possibly a retryable transient). Today both collapse to None and the resolver's "no fork point → pipeline_branch" path may fire incorrectly for legacy slices on a fresh worktree.

Holistic re-review (slice-4 coder v2, commit 2211f1c71f). Ran both BRC mandates over the +455/-188 delta across 4 modules.

### Mandate 1 — v1 issues addressed
My v1 verdict was ACK with non-blocking observations; the v2 delta did not need to address blockers from me, but several of my v1 notes were taken up:
- `_enumerate_contract_slices` silent `return []` paths now emit `logger.warning` on both the ImportError and contract-load-Exception arms (startup_reconciliation.py:462-481). My v1 silent-fallback concern is fixed.
- The plan-deviation around TASK-4-3 (v1 used an ls-remote proxy for "branch has commits", not merge-base) is now closed: v2 introduces `GatewayClient.merge_base` (gateway_client.py:2051-2123) and the resolver invokes it via `merge_base_lookup(integration_ref, derived_parent_ref)` with `refs/remotes/origin/...`-shaped refs. The merge-base SHA is logged for audit but not returned (downstream `is_slice_branch_merged_into_parent` / `create_slice_integration_branch` take branch names) — divergence from the plan's "use the merge-base SHA as the implicit parent" is explained explicitly in the v2 docstring and is the only coherent reading against the function's return type.
- TASK-4-4 cases 4/5 are now HITL-gated rather than OVERSEER_ALERT-broadcast (pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl + _escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl + _escalate_blocked_slice_to_hitl). The Decision lands on `contract.decisions` and gates phase completion via the existing `routes/phases.py:808` scanner.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v2 delta

**Pass 1 (end-to-end primary use case).** The crash-recovery walk strengthens in v2: the `resolve_worktree_path` fix means startup_reconciliation's per-slice tracker reconstruction (TASK-4-5 closure of #2409) now actually finds the contract instead of silently no-opping on `ContractNotFoundError`; restart_phase's per-slice tracker clear (TASK-4-1) likewise resolves the right path. Before v2 these were silent no-ops in production — a real Pass-1 break I missed in v1 because I traced the call chain logically without exercising the path resolution. The reviewer_code v1 NACK shape ("contract-path bug producing a silent no-op") is the exact shape this lens is supposed to catch; documenting the miss so my v3 watches for path-resolution producer/consumer asymmetry by default.

**Pass 2 (doc ↔ code symmetry).** Docstrings on `_resolve_slice_base_branch`, `_classify_non_complete_slice`, and the two HITL helpers were rewritten to match the new behaviour. One imprecision: the v2 proposal asserts "The HITL pauses the slice until the operator picks one of three options" — in code, the Decision gates the implement-phase completion scan (`routes/phases.py:808`), not the slice run loop. A corrupt-state slice is still re-yielded by the scheduler (or not, depending on `SliceStatus`) at runtime; only phase-complete blocks until the operator resolves the Decision. Operationally close enough that case 5 (corrupt) does pause the operator-visible pipeline state, but the verbiage "pauses the slice" is misleading. Non-blocking.

**Pass 3 (synthetic-key / sentinel coordination).**
- HITL Decision ID allocation in `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` uses `next_id = len(contract_local.decisions) + 1` with the `decision-N` prefix. The established orchestrator-side pattern is `next_cq_id(existing_decisions)` returning `cq-N` (see `impasse_routing.py:251-255` with explicit "Orchestrator-side HITL escalations write to the same `cq-N` namespace … so neither path collides with the pipeline-side `decision-N` allocator. See `shared/egg_contracts/decisions.py` (#2616).", and `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/sdlc.py:110`). The Decision schema docstring at `shared/egg_contracts/models.py:419-431` documents the split. Real collision probability is low (the bridge's `_sync_pipeline_decisions_to_contract` uses max+1 not len+1, and len+1 is gap-tolerant), but v2 deviates from the documented #2616 convention. Recommendation: replace with `from egg_contracts.decisions import next_cq_id; decision_id = next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions)`. Non-blocking but worth fixing.
- `mb_sha = "skipped"` literal-string sentinel mixed into the SHA-or-None return type from `merge_base_lookup` (pipelines.py:10527). Internal-only (the value never escapes `_resolve_slice_base_branch`), and the branches are explicit (`is None` vs `!= "skipped"`), so it works — but a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag would type cleaner. Style nit.
- Classification labels (fresh / resume / consensus_complete / blocked / corrupt) and tracker-key format `{pipeline_id}/{slice_id}` — producer/consumer coordination unchanged from v1, still symmetric.

**Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt).**
- `GatewayClient.merge_base` returns `None` on returncode 1 OR returncode 128 (the "unknown revision" path — refs not in local odb), conflating "no fork point exists" with "refs not yet fetched". For a legacy / orphaned non-root slice whose worktree's `refs/remotes/origin/<integration_branch>` is missing because the slice loop hasn't fetched yet, the resolver routes to `pipeline_branch` rather than the dependency-derived parent. Probability is low (legacy slices on a fresh worktree only, after slice-4 lands the eager-persist path is primary), and the operator can spot the gateway warning. Non-blocking.
- Layer-C tracker `evaluate()` failure → "resume" (consensus-incomplete) is the documented conservative direction with a logger.warning.
- `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` swallows exceptions with `logger.warning` and continues (the slice's contract status is preserved). The slice run loop will not silently treat it as resolved.
- The asymmetry doc-block comparing the resolver's probe-failure default ("has fork point → derived parent") and the classifier's probe-failure default ("fresh → re-yield READY") is exactly the kind of cross-module note the holistic lens hopes producers leave for the next reviewer.

### Non-blocking
- **pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl** — use `next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions)` instead of `f"decision-{len(contract_local.decisions) + 1}"`. The current ID allocation deviates from the documented #2616 convention and the impasse_routing precedent; replacing the four lines is a one-import fix.
- **pipelines.py:_resolve_slice_base_branch** — replace the `mb_sha = "skipped"` string-sentinel with a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag for type clarity.
- **pipelines.py:_classify_non_complete_slice case 4 (BLOCKED) scheduler unblock gap** — unchanged from v1 (still no `scheduler.mark_spawned` / `record_failure` for case 4; pure HITL gate). Theoretical today because `SliceStatus.BLOCKED` is not written anywhere by repo-wide grep, but if future code writes it, the scheduler will still re-yield BLOCKED slices whose parents are COMPLETE.
- **gateway_client.py:merge_base** — consider distinguishing returncode 1 (no fork point — genuine None) from returncode 128 (refs not present — possibly a retryable transient). Today both collapse to None and the resolver's "no fork point → pipeline_branch" path may fire incorrectly for legacy slices on a fresh worktree.

      \ doc-block comparing the resolver's probe-failure default (\"has fork point\
      \ \u2192 derived parent\") and the classifier's probe-failure default (\"fresh\
      \ \u2192 re-yield READY\") is exactly the kind of cross-module note the holistic\
      \ lens hopes producers leave for the next reviewer.\n\n### Non-blocking\n- **pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl**\
      \ \u2014 use `next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions)` instead of `f\"decision-{len(contract_local.decisions)\
      \ + 1}\"`. The current ID allocation deviates from the documented #2616 convention\
      \ and the impasse_routing precedent; replacing the four lines is a one-import\
      \ fix.\n- **pipelines.py:_resolve_slice_base_branch** \u2014 replace the `mb_sha\
      \ = \"skipped\"` string-sentinel with a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag for type\
      \ clarity.\n- **pipelines.py:_classify_non_complete_slice case 4 (BLOCKED) scheduler\
      \ unblock gap** \u2014 unchanged from v1 (still no `scheduler.mark_spawned`\
      \ / `record_failure` for case 4; pure HITL gate). Theoretical today because\
      \ `SliceStatus.BLOCKED` is not written anywhere by repo-wide grep, but if future\
      \ code writes it, the scheduler will still re-yield BLOCKED slices whose parents\
      \ are COMPLETE.\n- **gateway_client.py:merge_base** \u2014 consider distinguishing\
      \ returncode 1 (no fork point \u2014 genuine None) from returncode 128 (refs\
      \ not present \u2014 possibly a retryable transient). Today both collapse to\
      \ None and the resolver's \"no fork point \u2192 pipeline_branch\" path may\
      \ fire incorrectly for legacy slices on a fresh worktree."
    ack_version: 2
  version: 2
  slice_id: slice-4

### [2026-05-30T08:30:48Z] reviewer_code_holistic → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for coder

Holistic re-review (slice-4 coder v2, commit 2211f1c71f). Both BRC mandates over the +455/-188 delta.

### Mandate 1 — v1 issues addressed
My v1 was ACK with non-blocking observations. v2 takes several up:
- `_enumerate_contract_slices` silent `return []` now emits logger.warning on ImportError and contract-load-Exception arms (startup_reconciliation.py:462-481). v1 silent-fallback concern fixed.
- TASK-4-3 deviation (v1 used ls-remote proxy, not merge-base) closed: v2 adds `GatewayClient.merge_base` (gateway_client.py:2051-2123) and resolver invokes it via `merge_base_lookup(integration_ref, derived_parent_ref)`. Merge-base SHA is logged for audit but not returned (downstream consumers take branch names); divergence from plan's "use merge-base SHA as implicit parent" is explained in the v2 docstring.
- TASK-4-4 cases 4/5 now HITL-gated rather than OVERSEER_ALERT-broadcast. Decision lands on contract.decisions and gates phase completion via routes/phases.py:808 scanner.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v2 delta

**Pass 1 (end-to-end use case).** v2 strengthens the crash-recovery walk: resolve_worktree_path fix means startup_reconciliation per-slice tracker reconstruction (#2409 closure) now actually finds the contract instead of silently no-opping on ContractNotFoundError. Before v2 this was a silent no-op in production — a real Pass-1 break I missed in v1 by tracing the call chain logically without exercising the path resolution. The reviewer_code v1 NACK shape (contract-path bug producing silent no-op) is exactly what this lens should catch.

**Pass 2 (doc/code symmetry).** Docstrings on _resolve_slice_base_branch, _classify_non_complete_slice, the two HITL helpers were rewritten to match new behaviour. One imprecision: v2 proposal asserts "HITL pauses the slice until operator picks one of three options" — in code, Decision gates implement-phase completion scan (routes/phases.py:808), not slice run loop. Corrupt-state slice is still re-yielded by scheduler at runtime; only phase-complete blocks. Operationally close but verbiage "pauses the slice" is misleading.

**Pass 3 (synthetic-key coordination).**
- HITL Decision ID in _escalate_layer_c_hitl uses `next_id = len(contract_local.decisions) + 1` with `decision-N` prefix. Established orchestrator-side pattern is `next_cq_id()` returning `cq-N` (impasse_routing.py:251-255 with explicit "Orchestrator-side HITL escalations write to the same cq-N namespace … so neither path collides with the pipeline-side decision-N allocator. See shared/egg_contracts/decisions.py (#2616)."). Schema docstring at shared/egg_contracts/models.py:419-431 documents the split. Real collision probability is low but v2 deviates from #2616 convention.
- `mb_sha = "skipped"` literal-string sentinel mixed into SHA-or-None return type. Internal-only and explicit branches; works but probe_succeeded bool would type cleaner.
- Classification labels and tracker-key format unchanged from v1, still symmetric.

**Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt).**
- GatewayClient.merge_base returns None on returncode 1 OR returncode 128 (unknown revision — refs not in local odb). Conflates "no fork point" with "refs not fetched". For a legacy/orphaned non-root slice without fetched refs, resolver routes to pipeline_branch incorrectly. Edge case (legacy slices on fresh worktree only).
- Layer-C tracker.evaluate() failure → "resume" with logger.warning is correct conservative direction.
- _escalate_layer_c_hitl swallows exceptions with logger.warning; slice status preserved. Acceptable.
- Asymmetry doc-block comparing resolver vs classifier probe-failure defaults is exactly the cross-module note the holistic lens hopes producers leave.

### Non-blocking
- pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl — use next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions) instead of f"decision-{len(contract_local.decisions) + 1}". Deviates from documented #2616 convention; one-import fix.
- pipelines.py:_resolve_slice_base_branch — replace "skipped" string-sentinel with probe_succeeded: bool.
- pipelines.py:_classify_non_complete_slice case 4 BLOCKED — unchanged from v1; theoretical today since SliceStatus.BLOCKED is not written anywhere.
- gateway_client.py:merge_base — consider distinguishing returncode 1 (no fork point) from returncode 128 (refs not present, possibly retryable). Today both collapse to None.

### [2026-05-30T08:33:31Z] coder (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from coder

Slice-4 v3 (#2777) coder — addresses reviewer_code v2 blockers 1+2+3+4+5 AND reviewer_security v2 blocker.

**Addresses both reviewer_code v2 (5 findings) and reviewer_security v2 (1 finding):**

(A) reviewer_code v2 #1 + reviewer_security v2 #1 (THE SAME BLOCKER) — `_state_store_or_none()` silently no-op'd the HITL escalation. The helper called `get_state_store()` with no args, but the signature requires `repo_path`. The bare except swallowed the TypeError, the helper returned None, and the HITL short-circuited at `if store is None: return` — no Decision was ever appended. Fail-open of the operator-pause gate that reviewer_contract v1 NACKed me for. **Fixed by removing the helper entirely and threading `worktree_repo_path` + `current_phase` through `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` / `_escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl` / `_escalate_blocked_slice_to_hitl` from the Layer-C caller (which already has both in scope).**

(B) reviewer_code v2 #2 — Decision-ID collision via `len(decisions) + 1`. The `Decision.id` docstring explicitly flags this as the #2616 collision pattern. **Replaced with the canonical `next_cq_id` allocator** from `shared/egg_contracts/decisions.py` — orchestrator-side HITL escalations write to the `cq-N` namespace (the pipeline-side bridge owns `decision-N`).

(C) reviewer_code v2 #3 — Lock-nesting concern. Added a docstring block on `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` documenting the lock-nesting invariant (callers must NOT already hold the per-pipeline state lock when invoking this helper; today's only caller is the top-level Layer-C dispatch loop in `_run_implement_phase_slices` which is outside any nested lock).

(D) reviewer_code v2 #4 — Decision phase. **Replaced hard-coded `PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` with `current_phase or PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT`** so the Decision tracks the live phase.

(E) reviewer_code v2 #5 — merge-base call needs pre-fetch. My v2 invoked `merge_base_lookup(refs/remotes/origin/<branch>)` without first fetching the refs. Transient fetch lag → returncode 1 → None return → resolver silently routes onto `pipeline_branch`. **Fix: extended the `_probe_merge_base` callback to best-effort `fetch_branch` both refs before invoking merge-base.** Fetch failures are logged at debug; the merge-base call still runs against whatever the local odb has post-fetch.

(F) Non-blocking polish addressing all reviewer_code v2 and reviewer_security v2 non-blocking observations: (a) tightened `GatewayClient.merge_base` SHA shape check from `len >= 7 + hex` to `re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{40}")` via a new `_FULL_SHA_RE` constant. (b) Reverted ruff-format-introduced unparenthesised `except ValueError, TypeError:` clauses in `_lookup_open_pr` to parenthesised form (reviewer_code v2 non-blocking — unrelated to slice-4 scope). (c) Dropped the unused `context_prefix` parameter — the routing discriminator is now embedded in the question text (`[#2777 slice-4 TASK-4-4 case N]`).

Validation: ruff check + ruff format clean on all four files; AST parses; `scripts/check-file-sizes.py` exit 0. No tester-owned files modified.

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the v2 delta as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read the v2 diff as if you'd never seen v1. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the v2 delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has v2-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has v2-introduced races, contract has v2-introduced AC drift, code has v2-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did v1 issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does v2 introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, the v2 delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen the v2 diff with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from the v2 diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only your v2 diff with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

````yaml
id: 6a636d71-7b2e-4e
phase: implement
metadata:
  payload:
    summary: "Slice-4 v3 (#2777) coder \u2014 addresses reviewer_code v2 blockers\
      \ 1+2+3+4+5 AND reviewer_security v2 blocker.\n\n**Addresses both reviewer_code\
      \ v2 (5 findings) and reviewer_security v2 (1 finding):**\n\n(A) reviewer_code\
      \ v2 #1 + reviewer_security v2 #1 (THE SAME BLOCKER) \u2014 `_state_store_or_none()`\
      \ silently no-op'd the HITL escalation. The helper called `get_state_store()`\
      \ with no args, but the signature requires `repo_path`. The bare except swallowed\
      \ the TypeError, the helper returned None, and the HITL short-circuited at `if\
      \ store is None: return` \u2014 no Decision was ever appended. Fail-open of\
      \ the operator-pause gate that reviewer_contract v1 NACKed me for. **Fixed by\
      \ removing the helper entirely and threading `worktree_repo_path` + `current_phase`\
      \ through `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` / `_escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl` / `_escalate_blocked_slice_to_hitl`\
      \ from the Layer-C caller (which already has both in scope).**\n\n(B) reviewer_code\
      \ v2 #2 \u2014 Decision-ID collision via `len(decisions) + 1`. The `Decision.id`\
      \ docstring explicitly flags this as the #2616 collision pattern. **Replaced\
      \ with the canonical `next_cq_id` allocator** from `shared/egg_contracts/decisions.py`\
      \ \u2014 orchestrator-side HITL escalations write to the `cq-N` namespace (the\
      \ pipeline-side bridge owns `decision-N`).\n\n(C) reviewer_code v2 #3 \u2014\
      \ Lock-nesting concern. Added a docstring block on `_escalate_layer_c_hitl`\
      \ documenting the lock-nesting invariant (callers must NOT already hold the\
      \ per-pipeline state lock when invoking this helper; today's only caller is\
      \ the top-level Layer-C dispatch loop in `_run_implement_phase_slices` which\
      \ is outside any nested lock).\n\n(D) reviewer_code v2 #4 \u2014 Decision phase.\
      \ **Replaced hard-coded `PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` with `current_phase or PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT`**\
      \ so the Decision tracks the live phase.\n\n(E) reviewer_code v2 #5 \u2014 merge-base\
      \ call needs pre-fetch. My v2 invoked `merge_base_lookup(refs/remotes/origin/<branch>)`\
      \ without first fetching the refs. Transient fetch lag \u2192 returncode 1 \u2192\
      \ None return \u2192 resolver silently routes onto `pipeline_branch`. **Fix:\
      \ extended the `_probe_merge_base` callback to best-effort `fetch_branch` both\
      \ refs before invoking merge-base.** Fetch failures are logged at debug; the\
      \ merge-base call still runs against whatever the local odb has post-fetch.\n\
      \n(F) Non-blocking polish addressing all reviewer_code v2 and reviewer_security\
      \ v2 non-blocking observations: (a) tightened `GatewayClient.merge_base` SHA\
      \ shape check from `len >= 7 + hex` to `re.fullmatch(r\"[0-9a-f]{40}\")` via\
      \ a new `_FULL_SHA_RE` constant. (b) Reverted ruff-format-introduced unparenthesised\
      \ `except ValueError, TypeError:` clauses in `_lookup_open_pr` to parenthesised\
      \ form (reviewer_code v2 non-blocking \u2014 unrelated to slice-4 scope). (c)\
      \ Dropped the unused `context_prefix` parameter \u2014 the routing discriminator\
      \ is now embedded in the question text (`[#2777 slice-4 TASK-4-4 case N]`).\n\
      \nValidation: ruff check + ruff format clean on all four files; AST parses;\
      \ `scripts/check-file-sizes.py` exit 0. No tester-owned files modified."
    attestation: {}
    artifacts:
    - orchestrator/gateway_client.py
    - orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py
    risk_considered: "The `_state_store_or_none` fix is the production-correctness\
      \ fix \u2014 without it the entire HITL escalation surface was a fail-open.\
      \ The new threading of `worktree_repo_path` and `current_phase` through the\
      \ escalation helpers is purely additive; the helpers continue to swallow contract-load\
      \ failures with a logger.warning so a transient contract corruption does not\
      \ stop the slice loop. The merge-base pre-fetch is best-effort (fetch failures\
      \ are logged at debug and the merge_base call still runs) so a gateway hiccup\
      \ does not change the resolver's previous v2 behaviour. The `next_cq_id` adoption\
      \ is a no-op for fresh pipelines (cq-1) and harmless for in-flight pipelines\
      \ (the cq-N counter is gap-tolerant). No new trust-boundary surface."
    commit_sha: f856bc39b64dcd156f6b55ad6f19a7c51e9ac4f1

### [2026-05-30T08:35:57Z] reviewer_code_holistic → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for coder

Holistic re-review (slice-4 coder v3, commit f856bc39b). Both BRC mandates over the +152/-78 delta across pipelines.py and gateway_client.py.

### Mandate 1 — v2 issues addressed
- **My v2 non-blocking #1 (Decision-ID namespace deviation)** — fixed. `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` now imports `next_cq_id` from `egg_contracts.decisions` and allocates `cq-N` against `contract_local.decisions`. Docstring block at lines 10856-10865 explains the #2616 split. ✓
- **My v2 non-blocking #4 (`merge_base` returncode-128 conflation)** — mooted by the pre-fetch fix in the `_probe_merge_base` callback. Each ref is fetched via `spawner.gateway.fetch_branch(... args=[f"+refs/heads/{branch}:refs/remotes/origin/{branch}"])` before the merge-base call, so a transient "refs not in local odb" is no longer the silent driver of the None return. fetch_branch failures are debug-logged best-effort and the merge-base call still runs against whatever the local odb has post-fetch — the documented conservative semantic. ✓
- **My v2 non-blocking #2 (`mb_sha = "skipped"` string sentinel)** — UNCHANGED. Internal-only stylistic concern; not blocking. Acceptable.
- **My v2 non-blocking #3 (case 4 BLOCKED scheduler-unblock gap)** — UNCHANGED. Theoretical (still no repo write to `SliceStatus.BLOCKED`); future risk.

**Mandate 1 also has to include the v2 finding I missed.** The proposal calls out `_state_store_or_none()` as a silent fail-open: the helper called `get_state_store()` with no arg (signature requires `repo_path`), the bare `except Exception: return None` swallowed the TypeError, and the caller's `if store is None: return` short-circuited the whole HITL escalation. **The "operator pause" mechanism I ACK'd in v2 never actually wrote a Decision to the contract.** That is precisely the silent-fallback class my Pass 4 owns, and I missed it because I treated the helper as "lazy import + None-on-failure" stylistic boilerplate without tracing the signature. Logging the miss to recalibrate: when the lens is "does this path actually do what it claims end-to-end", helper bodies with bare-except-return-None deserve the same trace as their callers, especially when the caller's `if helper() is None: return` is the only mass-failure point.

v3 fixes this by removing `_state_store_or_none` entirely and threading `worktree_repo_path` + `current_phase` through from the Layer-C dispatch loop in `_run_implement_phase_slices` (which already has both in scope). The HITL path now executes end-to-end: `load_contract(pipeline_id, worktree_repo_path)` → `next_cq_id` → `append Decision` → `save_contract(... worktree_repo_path)`. ✓

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v3 delta

**Pass 1 (end-to-end primary use case).** Walked the corrupt-state path end-to-end on the v3 code: Layer-C dispatch loop reads `_current_phase = getattr(pipeline, "current_phase", None)` once, iterates `bootstrap_corrupt`, calls `_escalate_corrupt_slice_to_hitl(pipeline_id=..., slice_id=..., worktree_repo_path=worktree_repo_path, current_phase=_current_phase)` → `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` → contract write under the per-pipeline state lock → `routes/phases.py:808` scanner observes the unresolved `cq-N` decision when implement phase tries to complete → phase blocks awaiting operator input. The Pass-1 chain is now structurally correct end-to-end (and was not in v2). The merge-base callback also gains the fetch step so the "no fork point → pipeline_branch" arm is no longer reachable by a stale-odb path.

**Pass 2 (doc ↔ code symmetry).** The new docstring on `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` documents the lock-nesting invariant (callers must NOT already hold `get_pipeline_state_lock`). The `_probe_merge_base` callback's pre-fetch logic is annotated with reviewer_code v2 issue numbers and the rationale. The phase-parameterization is explained ("Layer C fires during bootstrap which can run before any phase walk, and future slice-DAG topologies may span phases"). Code-doc symmetry holds.

**Pass 3 (synthetic-key / sentinel coordination).**
- Decision ID: now `cq-N` via `next_cq_id(contract_local.decisions)` — matches the orchestrator-side convention from `impasse_routing.py:251-255`. ✓
- SHA shape: tightened to `_FULL_SHA_RE = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-f]{40}$")` with `.fullmatch(sha)` at gateway_client.py:2125. Rejects truncated / noisy stdout cleanly. ✓
- Ref-strip logic in `_probe_merge_base`: handles both `refs/remotes/origin/` and `refs/heads/` prefix shapes. The caller (resolver) only ever passes the `refs/remotes/origin/...` shape, so this is defence-in-depth.
- Question-text discriminator: `[#2777 slice-4 TASK-4-4 case N]` prefix is the new routing discriminator (replaces the dropped `context_prefix`). A future dispatcher in `routes/decisions.py` would match on the literal substring. The docstring on the two `_escalate_*_to_hitl` wrappers documents this contract.

**Pass 4 (silent-fallback hunt).**
- `_escalate_layer_c_hitl`: the v2 silent fail-open is gone. The remaining `except Exception` arms log `logger.warning` with `error=str(escalate_err)`. If contract load / save genuinely fails the Decision is not appended — operationally this means the corrupt slice doesn't pause, but the log line surfaces the failure. Acceptable; the alternative (raise) would brick the bootstrap.
- `_probe_merge_base` fetch failure: debug-logged (not warning) per the documented best-effort semantic. Merge-base runs against the local odb regardless. ✓
- ImportError on `egg_contracts.decisions` / `egg_contracts.loader`: logs warning and returns early. The HITL would silently skip if the egg_contracts package is unavailable, but the contract loader is also unavailable in that case so the slice-loop wouldn't proceed anyway. Acceptable.
- The `mb_sha = "skipped"` sentinel path still exists in `_resolve_slice_base_branch` (unchanged from v2). Style nit; not a silent fallback.

### Non-blocking
- **pipelines.py:_resolve_slice_base_branch** — `mb_sha = "skipped"` literal-string sentinel mixed into the SHA-or-None return type. Replace with a `probe_succeeded: bool` flag for type clarity. Carry-over from my v2 review.
- **pipelines.py:_classify_non_complete_slice case 4 BLOCKED** — scheduler-unblock gap unchanged. Theoretical today (no repo writes to `SliceStatus.BLOCKED`); future risk.
- **pipelines.py:_probe_merge_base fetch refspec construction** — strips two prefixes (`refs/remotes/origin/`, `refs/heads/`) but not `refs/tags/` or `refs/notes/` shapes. Caller only passes the `refs/remotes/origin/` shape so this is moot today; consider asserting on the prefix shape so a future caller can't silently produce a malformed refspec.
- **pipelines.py:_escalate_layer_c_hitl ImportError arm** — if `egg_contracts` is genuinely unimportable the HITL escalation logs and returns. Symmetric with the existing slice-loop import patterns; flagging for transparency.
