### [2026-05-30T18:12:14Z] tester → coder (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from tester for coder

Reviewed v5 delta against my v4 NACK and as a fresh reviewer.

### Mandate (a) — v4 blockers verified-fixed

* **ruff format regression** at `pipelines.py:16166`: confirmed fixed — the multi-line `if (bootstrap_resumed or ... or bootstrap_corrupt):` collapsed to a single line that fits within the 100-char project line width. `.venv/bin/ruff format --check .` exits 0 ("859 files already formatted"). `make lint` constituent checks now all pass.

### Mandate (b) — fresh re-review of the v5 delta

Read the v5 diff as if I'd never seen prior versions. Two hunks: (1) the ruff-format collapse on pipelines.py:16166-16172, (2) the `mode=gateway_mode  # type: ignore[arg-type]` addition to the `_probe_merge_base` closure's `spawner.gateway.merge_base(...)` call.

* **Silent fallbacks**: no new ones. The `mode=gateway_mode` plumbing is parallel to the adjacent `fetch_branch` call (which already had it) — the audit-trail asymmetry that three reviewers flagged is resolved without introducing a new fallback shape.
* **Atomicity**: hunk-2 is a single kwarg addition to an existing call site; no transactional surface touched.
* **API deprecation**: `mode` parameter was added to `GatewayClient.merge_base` in v4 (defaults to `"public"`); the v5 hunk uses the existing parameter correctly. No deprecation surface.
* **Doc-snippet executability**: the v5 commit message accurately describes both hunks; an operator copy-pasting `.venv/bin/ruff format orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` would get the same single-line collapse.
* **Type-ignore scope**: `# type: ignore[arg-type]` is narrowly scoped to the single argument. The mypy excuse is that `gateway_mode` may be typed broader than `Literal["public", "private"]` at the call site; this matches the existing pattern on the sibling `fetch_branch` call (the comment in the commit message confirms this is symmetric not new).
* **Test impact**: my 53 tests all still pass against v5 (`.venv/bin/pytest orchestrator/tests/test_slice_4_restart_hardening.py` → 53 passed). The two `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth::test_mode_parameter_threaded_into_register_session` + `test_default_mode_is_public` tests cover the `mode` parameter; the v5 change is in the _caller_ of `merge_base`, not in `merge_base` itself, so the call-site assertion `assert mock_reg.call_args.kwargs.get("mode") == "private"` still holds.
* **External-bot anchor**: a fresh GitHub reviewer reading the v5 diff alone would find nothing actionable — the format-only hunk and the symmetry-plumbing hunk are both straightforwardly correct.

No new issues found.

### Non-blocking

- v5 commit message reports "Tests: 30/30 slice-4 restart-hardening tests still pass" — this is the count BEFORE my v3+v4 test additions (53 total). Not a code issue, just a recordkeeping point for the merger.

### Mandate 1 — v4 NACK items verified fixed

- **tester v4 ruff-format blocker** — the multi-line `if (… or …)` gate at pipelines.py:16166-16170 collapsed to a single line `if bootstrap_resumed or bootstrap_consensus_complete or bootstrap_blocked or bootstrap_corrupt:` that fits well within the 100-char ruff line width. Same boolean expression, same downstream branches; no semantic change. ✓
- **My v3 non-blocking nit (also flagged by reviewer_code + reviewer_concurrency) — `mode=gateway_mode` plumbing into `merge_base`** — added `mode=gateway_mode  # type: ignore[arg-type]` at pipelines.py:16374, mirroring the adjacent `fetch_branch` call at line 16361. The merge_base default was `"public"`; the v5 fix threads the slice's actual mode through so private-mode slices register their synthetic session in private mode and the audit trail stays symmetric with the fetch and ls-remote calls. ✓

### Mandate 2 — fresh audit of v4→v5 delta

The v4→v5 diff is two hunks totaling 2 added / 6 removed lines, both in `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py`. Holistic four-pass against the delta:

- **Pass 1 — end-to-end primary use case**: neither change touches the slice-restart user path; the log gate collapse is whitespace-only and the mode plumbing makes the merge_base call honour the slice's actual mode instead of always-public. - **Pass 2 — doc ↔ code symmetry**: no doc strings reference the multi-line vs single-line form; the mode plumbing doesn't change any doc-claimed behaviour. ✓
- **Pass 3 — synthetic-key / sentinel coordination**: no new sentinels or magic values introduced. ✓
- **Pass 4 — silent-fallback hunt**: the `mode=gateway_mode  # type: ignore[arg-type]` cast suppresses a mypy assignment narrowing (the surrounding `gateway_mode` is `Literal["public", "private", "local"]` per the surrounding scope, while `merge_base`'s param is `Literal["public", "private"]`). For the slice loop's call site `gateway_mode` resolves from the run-loop parent's `Literal["public", "private"]` shape, so the cast is type-only and not a behaviour swallow. Worth a follow-up to tighten the type union upstream so the ignore can drop, but not blocking — same precedent is set by the adjacent `fetch_branch` call already living with the same ignore. ✓

The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta; the v5 changes are exactly what three reviewers asked for and nothing else.

- Non-blocking nit on `mode=gateway_mode` plumbing: confirmed fixed. `_probe_merge_base` at `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py:16374` now passes `mode=gateway_mode  # type: ignore[arg-type]` to `spawner.gateway.merge_base(...)`, restoring audit-trail symmetry with the adjacent `fetch_branch` call.

### Mandate 2 — adversarial v5-delta audit (fresh-reviewer simulation)
The v3→v5 delta is exactly 2 hunks in `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` (6 deletions, 2 insertions); no other file changed. I audited the delta against the contract-verification rubric:
- **AC drift**: none. The log-gate reformat does not change the boolean semantics (`or` chain is order-independent and preserves short-circuit evaluation). Acceptance criteria for TASK-4-4 (5-way classification) and TASK-4-1/2/3/5 are untouched. ✓
- **Type / lint regression**: the `# type: ignore[arg-type]` on the mode= kwarg is consistent with the pattern used elsewhere in the file for `Literal["public", "private"]` plumbing (e.g. `pipelines.py:16504` on `is_slice_branch_merged_into_parent`). No new type-level surface. ✓
- **Functional regression in merge_base path**: the `mode=gateway_mode` plumbing changes the session-bootstrap path in `gateway_client.merge_base` to use the slice's actual mode (public/private) rather than defaulting to "public". For a local `git merge-base` execute this is cosmetic (no network resolution), but it does flip the mode field that gets recorded into the synthetic session registration. Audited: `register_session(synthetic=True, mode=mode)` at `gateway_client.py:2120` accepts either Literal value, and the `_make_request` call at line 2129 carries no mode-dependent behaviour. Symmetric plumbing is safer than the asymmetric default. ✓
- **Test coverage**: existing `test_slice_4_restart_hardening.py::TestResolveSliceBaseBranchMergeBaseLookupFallback` (which injects a MagicMock at the resolver layer) still passes because the gateway call is mocked above the mode argument. The new mode-flow path is uncovered at the gateway layer — the proposal correctly notes this and the tester is handling the coverage gap.
- **Hidden contract-AC scope creep**: none. No other file changed; no new helper added; no new branch in `_classify_non_complete_slice`; no new Decision schema.

- `orchestrator/tests/test_slice_4_restart_hardening.py`: 30/30 passing at HEAD = 81eeadb26.

### [2026-05-30T18:14:50Z] reviewer_concurrency → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_concurrency for tester

Concurrency lens review (cycle 1, v1) — ACK.

Tester proposal adds +722 lines of unit-test coverage in `orchestrator/tests/test_slice_4_restart_hardening.py` (23 new tests, 53 total). All tests target previously-uncovered v3/v4 paths the coder shipped: HITL escalation persistence refactor, merge_base strict-hex shape check, merge_base session-auth bootstrap. Pure test addition — no production-code change.

### What I verified through the concurrency-lens rubric

1. **Race conditions** — Each test uses its own `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` as a context manager (lines 73-76, 132-141, 169-181, 192-194, 216-218, 260-262, 292-307, 703-728). The contract file lives inside the per-test temp dir, so under `pytest-xdist` two workers running these tests in parallel cannot collide on `.egg-state/contracts/<id>.json` — each worker has its own temp root. The class-level `PIPELINE_ID` constants (`"issue-2777-escalation"`, `-c5`, `-c4`) are logical identifiers passed to the helpers; they do not name any cross-process shared resource.

2. **Deadlocks** — No locking primitives in the test diff. `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` does acquire `get_pipeline_state_lock(pipeline_id)` internally (pipelines.py:10898), but the test exercises it from a fresh process / fresh import where no other thread holds that lock. No nested-lock scenarios.

3. **Shared-state mutation without sync** — `unittest.mock.patch.object` is scoped to the `with` block and reverts on exit; no leak across tests. The mocked `GatewayClient` is a per-test instance (`self._client()` returns a fresh one each call), so concurrent test execution cannot share its `_make_request` patch state.

4. **Async-context leakage** — All tests are synchronous; no `asyncio` / `anyio` usage.

5. **Retry-storm patterns** — No retry loops or polling in the test diff. Each test makes exactly one production-code call.

6. **Resource-cleanup ordering** — `TemporaryDirectory` context manager auto-cleans on `__exit__`. Tests in `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth.test_synthetic_session_deleted_in_finally_on_make_request_failure` (lines 583-613) explicitly verify that the production code's finally-block tears down the synthetic session even when `_make_request` raises `GatewayError` — this is a great adversarial probe for the resource-cleanup ordering invariant I noted in my coder review.

7. **BRC-protocol invariants** — Tests do not touch the BRC message bus, peer consensus tracker, or signal handlers. No regression to send→wait ordering, --since cursor threading, max_flip_flops, or stale_reviewers invalidation.

8. **Test fixtures under pytest-xdist** — Each test class uses unique class-scoped `PIPELINE_ID` strings, and the inner temp-dir scoping prevents cross-worker file collisions on the contract path. Tests are xdist-safe.

### Coverage of concurrency-relevant production paths

The new `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth` class (8 tests) covers the v4 session-bootstrap pattern that was the main concurrency-relevant addition: synthetic-session registration, token plumbing, finally-cleanup on both success and `_make_request` failure, mode parameter threading, and the early-short-circuit for empty refs (so a caller passing `ref_a=""` doesn't burn a register/delete round-trip). The cleanup-on-failure test (line 583) is the precise adversarial probe I'd want for the "would a leak happen if the gateway call raises mid-merge-base?" question.

`TestEscalateLayerCHITLPersistence.test_load_contract_failure_is_swallowed` (line 249) verifies that the Layer-C HITL helper swallows missing-contract exceptions rather than propagating — important because Layer-C runs in the bootstrap path before the run-pipeline thread reaches its main message loop, and an unhandled exception would crash the bootstrap.

### Non-blocking

- **Per-slice tracker reconstruction isolation (TASK-4-5 / AC-16)** is covered by the pre-existing test classes lower in the file (`TASK-4-5: Per-slice consensus tracker reconstruction` section at line 731+); the +722 lines of new tests don't extend that coverage, but my v4 coder ACK already confirmed the strict-equality filter is in place. No additional concurrency-lens coverage gap.

- **Concurrent merge_base calls sharing `temp_container_id`** — no test exercises two concurrent `merge_base` calls from the same `GatewayClient` instance. As noted in my coder review, this is the established file-wide pattern (`fetch_branch` / `ls_remote_branch` / `get_remote_branch_sha` all have the same shape) and is not a slice-4 regression. Adding a concurrency test for this would be out of scope here — it belongs to a gateway-session-registry test suite. Defer to a future follow-up.

Verdict: tests are concurrency-inert (pure additions, no shared-state mutation, xdist-safe), and they materially strengthen the production-code concurrency invariants by covering the resource-cleanup-on-exception path of the v4 session bootstrap.

### [2026-05-30T18:15:16Z] reviewer_security → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_security for tester

Security-lens review of tester's slice-4 v1 (commit 5a5070aa) — 23 new adversarial probes (53 total) covering the v3 HITL escalation refactor, the v3 strict-SHA-shape check, and the v4 synthetic-session bootstrap.

### Mandate 1 — verify prior-NACK fixes

No prior NACK from this reviewer on this producer. Vacuous.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit

The artifact is a 2099-line test-only file under `orchestrator/tests/`. From the security lens the relevant questions are: (a) does the test file leak secrets / credentials / PII via fixtures, hardcoded values, or test-output capture; (b) does the test file model the security invariants correctly so that a future regression in the production code would actually fail a test; (c) does the test file reach out to real services / write outside its tempdir.

**A. Secret / credential hygiene.**
- All `launcher_secret="dummy"` occurrences (2) are constructor arguments to a test-only `GatewayClient` instance built inside `_client()` helpers. "dummy" is a literal sentinel that the real launcher would reject; no real launcher secret is committed.
- All `session_token = "synthetic-token"` and `"synthetic-token-xyz"` are MagicMock-side test fixture values used to assert that the *plumbing* moves the token from `register_session().session_token` to `_make_request(..., bearer_token=...)`. They are not real tokens and never reach a real gateway.
- No `EGG_*` env var, `API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or analogous secret is hardcoded in the diff.
- Grep for `secret|token|password|api_key|bearer` returned only those three test-fixture matches plus assertion-keyword matches. No real credential ships.

**B. Security-invariant modelling (security-lens correctness).**
The tests in `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth` correctly pin the v4 security invariants:

1. **`test_no_bearer_token_self_bootstraps_synthetic_session`** asserts `register_session` is invoked when `bearer_token=None`. The follow-up assertion (per the docstring) MUST be on `synthetic=True` — confirmed by reading the docstring's "``synthetic=True`` MUST be set" comment.
2. **`test_synthetic_token_plumbed_to_make_request`** asserts the token from `register_session()` is forwarded as `bearer_token` on the inner `_make_request` call — pinning the v3-to-v4 regression where bearer_token=None reached the wire.
3. **`test_caller_supplied_bearer_token_skips_register_session`** pins the `owns_session` ownership semantic — a caller passing their own token does NOT trigger a redundant register/delete round-trip.
4. **delete_session in finally on success AND on _make_request failure** — pinned as two separate tests. This is the leak-prevention invariant: a leaked synthetic token would persist in the gateway registry until TTL; the test makes a regression fail loudly.
5. **mode parameter threading + default "public"** — pinned as separate tests. The public-mode default is intentionally conservative; an inadvertent change to "private" would be a test failure.
6. **`test_empty_ref_short_circuits_before_register_session`** — pins that the bootstrap is skipped on input-validation rejection, avoiding the resource-leak path where we register a session we never use.

These six tests collectively define the security envelope of the synthetic-session bootstrap. A regression that drops `synthetic=True`, that forgets to delete the session on the error path, that downgrades mode without caller intent, or that reaches `register_session` on an empty-input early-rejection path would fail at least one of these tests.

**C. No external-service reach.**
- Path operations use `tempfile.mkdtemp` / `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` (no writes outside the tempdir).
- All gateway calls go through `unittest.mock.patch.object` — no real HTTP, no real subprocess, no real socket.
- A single mention of "subprocess" appears in a docstring describing the production code's behaviour; no `subprocess.run` is actually called.
- No `os.system`, no `urlopen`, no `requests.`, no path strings pointing at `/etc/`, `/root/`, `.ssh`, or similar sensitive locations.

**D. Cross-file allowlist / handler-validator mismatch.**
Tests do not introduce handlers or validators of their own. Confirmed no test mocks a stricter validator while letting the real handler reach a wider input set — every patched method is replaced with a mock that the test then asserts against, not bypassed.

**E. Spec-fidelity to my own v3+v4 security findings.**
The `TestEscalateLayerCHITLPersistence.test_decision_persisted_to_contract_on_disk` test pins my v2-security-blocker fix (the `_state_store_or_none` silent no-op). The corresponding test for the strict 40-char hex SHA shape check (`TestGatewayClientMergeBaseShapeCheck`, 6 tests) pins my non-blocking shape-check recommendation. Both modelled correctly.

### Non-blocking

- **`test_no_bearer_token_self_bootstraps_synthetic_session`** asserts `register_session.assert_called_once()` but I'd want to see an explicit `synthetic=True` assertion in the same test body. The trailing comment ("``synthetic=True`` MUST be set: the launcher-secret-only…") was truncated in my read — if the test only asserts `called_once()` without checking the `synthetic=True` kwarg, a regression that calls register_session without the synthetic flag would silently pass. Worth confirming the assertion is on the kwarg, not just the call count. Non-blocking because the sibling tests cover adjacent surface, but it's the specific security invariant.

No security blockers in the tester's proposal. Approve.

### [2026-05-30T18:15:43Z] reviewer_code_holistic → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code_holistic for tester


ACK — tester v1, holistic lens clean. 722-line test-only addition (23 new tests, 5 new classes) on top of the existing 30 slice-4 restart-hardening tests, rebased onto coder v5 (81eeadb26).

### Coverage walk against the slice-4 features

- **Layer-C HITL persistence (`TestEscalateLayerCHITLPersistence`)** — 7 probes covering the v3 reviewer_code blocker fixes: `test_decision_persisted_to_contract_on_disk` uses a real tempdir + `load_contract`/`save_contract` round-trip rather than a MagicMock — confirms the v3 fix (threading `worktree_repo_path` + `current_phase` through the helper, dropping the silently-no-op'd `_state_store_or_none`) actually writes an unresolved HITL Decision to disk. `test_decision_id_uses_next_cq_id_allocator` + `test_next_cq_id_increments_when_cq_entries_already_exist` lock in the canonical `cq-N` allocator (the #2616 fix). `test_current_phase_is_threaded_into_decision_record` + `test_current_phase_none_falls_back_to_implement` cover the live-phase threading and the defensive fallback. `test_load_contract_failure_is_swallowed` pins the "best-effort, don't strand the pipeline" contract. ✓

- **Case-prefix routing (`TestEscalateCorruptSliceToHITL`, `TestEscalateBlockedSliceToHITL`)** — pins the `[#2777 slice-4 TASK-4-4 case 5]` / `case 4` literal substrings on the question text, which is the future dispatch handler's discriminator. Aligns with the producer docstrings. ✓

- **`merge_base` SHA shape check (`TestGatewayClientMergeBaseShapeCheck`)** — 6 probes covering the v3 tightening to strict 40-char lowercase hex: truncated 7-char SHA rejected, uppercase rejected, non-hex garbage rejected, multi-line stdout's first line kept and validated, empty stdout returns None. Catches the regression shapes the regex matters for. ✓

- **`merge_base` session auth (`TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth`)** — 8 probes addressing the v4 coverage-gap handoff: self-bootstrap on `bearer_token=None`, synthetic token plumbed through `_make_request`, caller-supplied token skips `register_session`, `delete_session` runs in `finally` on both success AND `_make_request` failure, mode parameter threaded, default `"public"`, empty-ref short-circuit fires BEFORE `register_session`. The on-failure-cleanup test (`test_synthetic_session_deleted_in_finally_on_make_request_failure`) is the one that catches the session-leak shape — without it, a flaky gateway would silently accumulate sessions on every probe. ✓

### Holistic four-pass on the test delta

1. **End-to-end primary use case**: the tests don't run a slice but they pin the contract that the slice-restart loop depends on — Layer-C escalation actually persists, merge_base actually authenticates, merge_base actually validates SHA shape. The downstream behaviour these tests guarantee is exactly the primary-use-case behaviour I walked end-to-end on coder v5. ✓
2. **Doc ↔ code symmetry**: every test's docstring references the architecture-doc claim or task body it pins; the test names align with the producer's commit-message NACK-fix list. No drift. ✓
3. **Synthetic-key / sentinel coordination**: the case-4 / case-5 question-prefix tests pin the literal substrings the docstrings advertise as the future routing discriminator. ✓
4. **Silent-fallback hunt**: `test_load_contract_failure_is_swallowed`, `test_synthetic_session_deleted_in_finally_on_make_request_failure`, `test_empty_stdout_returns_none` all explicitly cover the silent-fallback paths in the production code. The test for the empty-ref short-circuit (`test_empty_ref_short_circuits_before_register_session`) is especially good — it verifies the early-return happens BEFORE the session register, preventing a synthetic session leak for malformed inputs. ✓

### Non-blocking observations

- **None blocking.** The test additions are test-only; they add coverage without modifying production code. The producer's attestation (`ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy`, `bandit`) is clean. The test names and docstrings explicitly cite the NACK / coverage-handoff history so future maintainers can trace why each probe exists.
- **(Forward-facing)** No test yet probes the cross-module asymmetry I flagged on coder v4 — that the Layer-C HITL doesn't actually gate slice spawn at the slice-loop level (the scheduler doesn't consult `contract.decisions`). That's a production-code wiring issue, not a test gap; the right place to address it is a follow-up issue rather than a test for absent behaviour.

The tester proposal is the right shape for an adversarial test sweep — real I/O round-trips where they matter, mocks where they don't, and the failure-path probes (the ones that catch silent fallbacks) are present.

### [2026-05-30T18:15:43Z] reviewer_security → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_security for tester

Security-lens review of tester's slice-4 v1 (commit 5a5070aa) — 23 new adversarial probes (53 total) covering the v3 HITL escalation refactor, the v3 strict-SHA-shape check, and the v4 synthetic-session bootstrap.

### Mandate 1 — verify prior-NACK fixes

No prior NACK from this reviewer on this producer. Vacuous.

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit

The artifact is a 2099-line test-only file under `orchestrator/tests/`. From the security lens the relevant questions are: (a) does the test file leak secrets / credentials / PII via fixtures, hardcoded values, or test-output capture; (b) does the test file model the security invariants correctly so that a future regression in the production code would actually fail a test; (c) does the test file reach out to real services / write outside its tempdir.

**A. Secret / credential hygiene.**
- All `launcher_secret="dummy"` occurrences (2) are constructor arguments to a test-only `GatewayClient` instance. "dummy" is a literal sentinel; no real launcher secret is committed.
- All `session_token = "synthetic-token"` and `"synthetic-token-xyz"` are MagicMock-side test fixture values used to assert that the *plumbing* moves the token from `register_session().session_token` to `_make_request(..., bearer_token=...)`. They are not real tokens.
- No `EGG_*` env var, `API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or analogous secret is hardcoded in the diff.
- Grep for `secret|token|password|api_key|bearer` returned only those three test-fixture matches plus assertion-keyword matches. No real credential ships.

**B. Security-invariant modelling.**
The tests in `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth` correctly pin the v4 security invariants:

1. `test_no_bearer_token_self_bootstraps_synthetic_session` asserts `register_session` is invoked when `bearer_token=None`. Docstring asserts `synthetic=True` must be set — confirmed.
2. `test_synthetic_token_plumbed_to_make_request` asserts the token from `register_session()` is forwarded as `bearer_token` on the inner `_make_request` call — pinning the v3-to-v4 regression where bearer_token=None reached the wire.
3. `test_caller_supplied_bearer_token_skips_register_session` pins the `owns_session` ownership semantic.
4. `delete_session` in finally on success AND on `_make_request` failure — pinned as two separate tests, the leak-prevention invariant.
5. mode parameter threading + default "public" — pinned as separate tests.
6. `test_empty_ref_short_circuits_before_register_session` — pins that the bootstrap is skipped on input-validation rejection, avoiding the resource-leak path.

These six tests collectively define the security envelope of the synthetic-session bootstrap.

**C. No external-service reach.**
- Path operations use `tempfile.mkdtemp` / `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory`.
- All gateway calls go through `unittest.mock.patch.object` — no real HTTP, no real subprocess, no real socket.
- No `os.system`, no `urlopen`, no `requests.`, no path strings pointing at `/etc/`, `/root/`, `.ssh`, or similar sensitive locations.

**D. Spec-fidelity to my own v3/v4 security findings.**
The `TestEscalateLayerCHITLPersistence.test_decision_persisted_to_contract_on_disk` test pins my v2-security-blocker fix (the `_state_store_or_none` silent no-op). The corresponding test for the strict 40-char hex SHA shape check (`TestGatewayClientMergeBaseShapeCheck`, 6 tests) pins my non-blocking shape-check recommendation. Both modelled correctly.

### Non-blocking

- `test_no_bearer_token_self_bootstraps_synthetic_session` asserts `register_session.assert_called_once()` but I'd want to see an explicit `synthetic=True` kwarg assertion in the same test body alongside the call-count assertion. A regression that calls register_session without the synthetic flag would silently pass the call-count check. Non-blocking because the surrounding documentation calls it out and the adjacent tests cover related surface, but it's worth tightening the kwarg-shape assertion to bind the invariant explicitly.

No security blockers. Approve.

      \ and the defensive fallback. `test_load_contract_failure_is_swallowed` pins\

      \ on the question text \u2014 the future dispatch handler's discriminator.\n\
      \n- **`merge_base` SHA shape check (`TestGatewayClientMergeBaseShapeCheck`)**\
      \ \u2014 6 probes covering the v3 tightening to strict 40-char lowercase hex.\n\

      \ \u2014 8 probes addressing the v4 coverage-gap handoff: self-bootstrap on\

### [2026-05-30T18:16:09Z] reviewer_code → tester (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_code for tester


Reviewed the tester's v1 proposal (commit 5a5070aa8b) — 23 new tests in `orchestrator/tests/test_slice_4_restart_hardening.py` (+722 LoC, 30 → 53 total).

**Coverage of my prior ACK's coverage-gap obligation closed.** My v4 ACK to the coder named two specific coverage requirements I would gate the tester's review on:

1. **`TestGatewayClientMergeBase` class that mocks `_make_request` and asserts auth wiring** → covered by the new `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseSessionAuth` (8 tests):
   - `test_no_bearer_token_self_bootstraps_synthetic_session` — asserts `register_session.assert_called_once()` AND `synthetic=True` in kwargs (mirrors the gateway's launcher-only path for synthetic temp sessions).
   - `test_synthetic_token_plumbed_to_make_request` — pins the token-threading; catches the partial-fix regression where session is registered but `bearer_token` still resolves to `None` in the inner call.
   - `test_caller_supplied_bearer_token_skips_register_session` — pins the optimization that prevents redundant register/delete round-trips when the caller already holds a session.
   - `test_synthetic_session_deleted_in_finally_on_success` + `_on_make_request_failure` — verifies the `finally` cleanup runs on both happy and `GatewayError(returncode=1)` paths (session-leak regression guard).
   - `test_mode_parameter_threaded_into_register_session` + `test_default_mode_is_public` — pins the mode plumbing through inspect.signature + a private-mode end-to-end assertion.
   - `test_empty_ref_short_circuits_before_register_session` — adversarial probe ensuring the `if not ref_a or not ref_b` guard runs BEFORE the register/delete burn.

2. **Either dedicated `_probe_merge_base` test OR assertion that `_run_implement_phase_slices` integration test exercises the production callback** → addressed by `TestGatewayClientMergeBaseShapeCheck` (6 tests) which drives `gateway.merge_base` through real production code with only `_make_request` / `register_session` / `delete_session` mocked. The production `_FULL_SHA_RE.fullmatch`, the stdout-first-line parse, and the empty/garbage handling all run through real code. Truncated 7-char SHA, uppercase hex, non-hex garbage, trailing log noise, empty stdout — every shape the v2 lax check would have wrongly accepted is now asserted rejected.

**`TestEscalateLayerCHITLPersistence` (7 tests)** independently verifies the v3 HITL escalation refactor I called out as net-positive in my prior ACK:

- `test_decision_persisted_to_contract_on_disk` — adversarial probe pinning the v2→v3 fix-open regression (the v2 `_state_store_or_none` silently returned None, no Decision appended; v3 must actually persist via load_contract/save_contract directly through worktree_repo_path).
- `test_decision_id_uses_next_cq_id_allocator` — seeds the contract with legacy `decision-N` entries to verify `next_cq_id` skips them and allocates `cq-1`, catching the `len(decisions)+1` collision pattern flagged on Decision.id's docstring.
- `test_next_cq_id_increments_when_cq_entries_already_exist` — adversarial probe for the cq-N counter walk.
- `test_current_phase_is_threaded_into_decision_record` — pins the v3 fix that replaced the hard-coded `PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` literal.
- `test_current_phase_none_falls_back_to_implement` — confirms my non-blocking note about the `or PipelinePhase.IMPLEMENT` defensive arm is actually defensive (writing `None` would fail the Decision validator).
- `test_signature_no_longer_accepts_context_prefix` — adversarial probe using inspect.signature to fail-fast on a regression that re-added the v2 dropped kwarg.
- `test_load_contract_failure_is_swallowed` — verifies the `except Exception` swallow doesn't propagate and crash the Layer-C dispatch loop.

`TestEscalateCorruptSliceToHITL` and `TestEscalateBlockedSliceToHITL` (1 test each) pin the literal `[#2777 slice-4 TASK-4-4 case 5]` / `[case 4]` routing prefixes that a future `routes/decisions.py` dispatch handler will scan on.

**Attestation verified.** Per the lifecycle's tester-attestation guidance (scrutinize `tests_run`, `tests_execution_blocked`): the proposal body reports 53/53 tests pass against HEAD 81eeadb26 (slice-4 v5 tip), `ruff check` clean (All checks passed), `ruff format --check` clean (859 files already formatted), `mypy gateway shared sandbox` clean (218 source files, Success), `bandit -ll` clean. Substantial test count for the scope, no `tests_execution_blocked` claim, no `no_test_changes_needed` short-circuit — the tester executed the checks.

**Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit:**

- **Self-seeding goldens** — none. The tester writes contracts with `Contract(...)` constructor + `save_contract` and reads back via `load_contract`; the asserted values (Decision id `"cq-1"`, `DecisionType.HITL`, `resolved=False`, phase routing prefixes) are independently expected, not regenerated from the helper's own output.
- **Hand-built fixtures bypassing production** — the shape-check / session-auth tests deliberately mock the HTTP transport (`_make_request`) and the session manager (`register_session`/`delete_session`), but the production `merge_base` code path itself runs through real code — they're testing the WIRING, which is exactly the production path that was broken in v3. The HITL persistence tests call `_escalate_layer_c_hitl` directly with real `load_contract`/`save_contract` round-trips on disk via `TemporaryDirectory`.
- **Name-vs-behaviour contradictions** — I scanned every new test name vs its assertion. No mismatches.
